14 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [jAN.-MAR., (Received 16th April 1892.) CUSTOM HOUSE, CHEFOO, x2th April 1892. SIR, I.&mdash;In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circulars Nos. 21 of 1869, 14 of 1872, and 6 of 1875, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the first quarter of the year 1892. Remarks.&mdash;During January the highest reading of the barometer was 30.588, on the 21st; and the lowest 30.052, on the 15th. The highest temperature was 8i&deg;, on the 27th; and the lowest 4i&deg;.5, on the 7th. n.w. winds prevailed. Rain fell on 2 days. During February the highest reading of the barometer was 30.452, on the 16th; and the lowest 29.760, on the 22nd. The highest temperature was 82&deg;, on the 23rd; and the lowest 450, on the 16th. n.e. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 9th, averaging 20.1 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 10 days. During March the highest reading of the barometer was 30.374, on the 19th; and the lowest 29.770, on the 5th. The highest temperature was 82&deg;, on the 5th; and the lowest 38&deg;, on the 16th. N.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 18th, averaging 16.2 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 15 days, measuring 2.56 inches. IX. I I . PART IV. NOTIFICATIONS, During the Quarter ended March 31, 1892. &#39;
160 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [jAN.-MAR., 1892.] NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 253. SHANGHAI DISTRICT. SOUTHERN ENTRANCE TO THE YANGTZE. Notice is hereby given that on account of alterations which lfave taken place in the bed of the river, as disclosed by a recent survey, the following changes have been made in the marking of the channel. The lightship Kiutoan has been shifted to a position off the south-eastern end of Block&not; house Shoal from which the Kiutoan Beacon bears S. 68|&deg; W., distant about 3miles. The Middle Ground Lower Buoy (No. 2 in the published List for 1891) has been shifted to a spot from which House Island Surveying Beacon bears N. ii&deg; W., distant about 3^ miles, &quot;where it marks the port side of the channel entering. This buoy is now painted black, and surmounted by a black spherical cage. The Middle Ground Upper Buoy (No. 3 in the published List for 1891) has been shifted to a spot from which Kiutoan Beacon bears S. 12&deg; W., distant about 2^ miles, where it marks the port side of the channel entering and the north-western end of the long tongue or shoal into which the former Middle Ground has developed. This buoy is painted black, and surmounted by a black cylindrical cage. A red buoy, surmounted by a black spherical cage, to be known as the South-east Knoll Buoy, has been moored [with small Kiutoan Beacon S. 87&deg; W., distant about 2^ miles] off the south-easternmost of a line of knolls which have formed off the south-western side of Blockhouse Shoal. This buoy marks the starboard side of the channel entering. All bearings are magnetic. CAUTION. Vessels should not attempt to pass to the north-eastward of the Kiutoan lightship in her present position. Shanghai, 3rd March 1892.
